### Flattening via Iterative Proportional Fitting (IPF) / Sinkhorn

We can frame as: We seek p such that p*p = q where q as flat as possible with max M. Alternatively, given a target q_k, find p to match. The KKT fixed point can be solved by multiplicative updates similar to scaling for contingency tables.

Consider p_i >0. Let c = p*p. We want all c_k ≤M. Suppose choose M and select active coefficients. There is a "volume maximizing" strategy: maximize ∑p subject to p*p≤M. This is nonconvex but maybe can be solved via iterative scaling:
For current M and p, compute ratios r_k = (p*p)_k / M. We want r_k ≤1. To increase feasible volume, adjust p_i by multiplier u_i such that new p_i = u_i p_i. Then new c_k ≈ u-shift? If we ignore cross terms, choose u_i proportional to 1/√(∑ w_k u_{k-i} p?) Not.

Maybe use "AltMin": fix q = current c normalized, update p via square root? If q has coefficients, we want a polynomial A with A^2≈q. Nonnegative A. Taking square root in time domain? Not simple.

### Use Convex Optimization on log-convex / Majorization

### Hybrid Search Plan

Given 2400s, we can do:
1. Define `evaluate_fft(p, n)` returns J using FFT full convolution and max. Can call many times.
2. Define `smooth_step(p, tau, betas)` uses FFT weights and multiplicative update, line search.
3. Run several individual optimizations:
   - Start uniform: schedule tau from low to high, multiple cycles, random perturbations.
   - Start from parametric shapes, optimize via mirror descent.
   - Use random initial vectors from low-pass noise and local descent.
   - Maybe use simulated annealing: if no improvement for a while, add random perturbation (maintain sum) and continue.
4. Keep global best p (by FFT score; final exact).
5. At end, exact evaluate best (and maybe exact-evaluate a small set of top candidates) and return best list.

Need choose hyperparameters robustly. Since we can't test, must be conservative to not spend all time. We can allocate time budgets.

Simpler robust approach: Use `scipy.optimize.minimize` on a lower-dimensional parameterization! This may be more reliable than full vector gradient. We can parameterize p_i = softmax(W[i] + params*basis[i])? But objective nonconvex and high-dim. However we can optimize a few coefficients of a polynomial/spline profile, then refine with mirror descent.

### Population / Differential Evolution

Because objective rugged, we can maintain population of size P (e.g., 20-50) length n. Evaluate each using FFT (fast). For each, generate offspring via crossover/mutation (mix, perturb, self-mix). Prefer lower score. This global search may escape local minima. Time budget: P*iterations * FFT O(n log n). For n=4096, FFT 8192 maybe 0.2ms; 100k offspring ->20s? Actually Python loops overhead bigger. Could do 10k offspring -> few seconds. Good. We can combine with local improvement.

But random mixing of probabilities may produce high peaks; need smart mutation. Since optimum likely smooth, use low-pass mutations: add small random smooth noise (e.g., FFT random phases with low amplitude) to p, or mix two good parents and smooth. Differential evolution: p_child = p_best + F*(p_r1 - p_r2), project to simplex (clip, normalize). This can explore. Use tight F to not destroy features. Keep best. At end refine best with mirror descent.

But population search over 4k dims with only 1 CPU 2400s: 10k evals easy. We can evaluate many.

### Core objective evaluation without exact evaluator

Implement `score_fft(a)` for unnormalized or normalized. To avoid repeated normalization, maintain p sum1. `a` nonnegative. If sum too small invalid. score = 2n*max(conv(p,p)). Since sum1.
FFT convolution:
```
def conv_full(a, n):
    L = 1 << ((2*n-2).bit_length())
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0)  # FFT negative tiny
    return c
```
For n up to 16384, L=32768, ok. For multiple n, precompute L. `np.fft` uses threads? It may use multiple? We have 1 CPU but okay. Could set `numpy.seterr`?

Need ensure objective from FFT matches exact evaluator within small tolerance. FFT double errors ~1e-12 relative, ok. At final exact evaluate.

### Smoothing weights

For q, use c from FFT. If τ is small, q~uniform over all lags. If τ large, q~peak. Let's use parameter `temp = M * alpha`, where M=max c. x=(c-M)/temp; q = softmax x. alpha controls active width. α=0.01 => only very near peak; 0.1 => 10% width; 1 => all. We can schedule over iterations.
Actually c values range [0,M]. Use `scale = max(1e-15, M*alpha)`. Then q=exp((c-M)/scale)/sum.

Update using d. We need choose β. We can use multiplicative update with ratio r_i = d_i / max(p_i, floor). p_new = p / (r + rmin?)^β? If r varies. Another simpler update: p_new = p ** (1+β) / d^β. Because r = d/p; p / r^β = p / (d/p)^β = p^{1+β}/d^β. This has dimension: d^β units? p sum1, d sum~1; p^{1+β}/d^β may underflow. Use normalized:
```
logp = beta * log(p_safe) - beta * log(d_safe)
p_new = exp(logp - max(logp)); p_new /= sum
```
This is p / (d/p)^β. If β=1, p_new ∝ p^2/d. It can amplify edges. For stability, β in [0.01,0.5].
Alternatively, `p_new = p * (mean_d / d_safe)^β` and normalize. This avoids log; if d small. Use d_safe = max(d, floor_d). mean_d = d.mean() (or 1/n). Then factor f=(mean(d)/d)^β. For high d, f<1; for low, >1. This is like Euclidean multiplicative with log. It may be better: p_new = p * exp(-β * log(d/mean_d)). Equivalent to p * (mean_d/d)^β.
Since d_i has same scale, factor dimensionless. Update sum normalized.

Let's test at uniform with d variation: edges d lower -> factor >1, edges increase; center d higher -> factor <1. Good.
β can be small to avoid oscillation. Line search over β [0.05,0.1,...,1].
We can also combine with Euclidean: p_new = normalize(p + β*(mean_d - d))? But p_i O1/n, d O1/n; β O100? Multiplicative likely stable.

We need ensure p remains nonnegative; with floor.

### Search at runtime: Continuous profile optimization

Let's design a robust profile evaluator:
- Choose n_test maybe 2048 for profile search (fast), then refine at target n.
- Candidate U array length n (raw heights, sum not necessarily normalized). Score = 2n*max(conv(U,U)) / sum(U)^2. We can evaluate raw directly; no need normalize. For profile U_i = max(0, f(x_i))? If f has negative values, clip to 0? But clipping changes. Better ensure f positive: e.g., exponential of polynomial: U_i = exp(∑ θ_k B_k(x_i)). This is positive, flexible, can create tilts. Then normalize if desired. Use FFT score. Exponential can be very peaked; good.

Parameterize log U = θ0 * x + θ1*(2x-1)^2 + θ2*cos(π*x) + ... to allow asymmetric smooth profiles. Since scale invariant, we can drop constant by subtract max. For each θ vector, compute U=exp(poly), score. We can optimize θ using random/coordinate/Nelder-Mead. The exponent range should be limited to avoid zero (but exponential positive). U can have large endpoint ratio. Could use softplus? exp enough.

Basis functions for asymmetry: x, x^2, x^3 maybe; oscillatory cos(πx), cos(2πx), sin. We can use polynomial up to degree 4 in x: U=exp(∑_{1..D} θ_k (x^k - mean? maybe)). Nonnegative. For small θ, uniform. For larger, asymmetric.

Coordinate descent: For each parameter θ_j, scan a small grid around current (e.g., ±step) to improve score. Since objective smooth, step maybe 0.1. Use multiple random initial θ.

But a full 7-D profile may not capture all optimal details. After profile, full vector mirror descent can add high frequency.

### Mirror Descent on Full Vector (asymmetric)

Need implement carefully.

Pseudo:
```
def refine(p, n, time_limit, tau_schedule, beta_values):
    best_p = p.copy(); best_score = score_fft(p)
    current = p.copy()
    no_improve = 0
    while time left:
       tau = current_tau
       c = conv_full_fft(current)
       M = c.max()
       scale = max(M*tau, 1e-15)
       q = exp((c-M)/scale); q/=q.sum()
       d = corr_valid_fft(q, current)
       mean_d = d.mean()
       improved = False
       for beta in betas:
          floor = max(1e-12, current.min())? d_safe = np.maximum(d, floor_d)
          factor = (mean_d / d_safe) ** beta
          cand = current * factor
          cand += 1e-15
          cand /= cand.sum()
          s = score_fft(cand)
          if s < best_score: update best
          if s < current_score - 1e-12: current=cand; improved=True; current_score=s; break
       if not improved:
          # perturb or change tau
          if tau maybe schedule; add small noise; accept if not too worse?
```
But scoring cand via FFT each beta (say 5) per iteration. 1000 iterations ->5000 FFT, ok.

Step `factor = (mean_d/d)^β` can be unstable if d very small (ends) -> huge factor, making some entries dominate. We can cap factor: `factor = np.clip(factor, 0.1, 10)` for β line search. Also use `d_safe = np.maximum(d, 1e-12)`.
If current has zeros, d for zero p may be >0; factor large, re-introduce. Good.

We can also try inverse update? Our update reduces high d. But if d indicates gradient, maybe we need to increase high d? Let's verify. We want lower peak c_k. d_i = ∑ q_k p_{k-i}; gradient of ∑ q c is 2d. To minimize, we should decrease p_i where d_i high. factor=(mean/d)^β does that. Good.

Line search β: small to large. For best improvement, choose min among several. If all worse, current_score might be local.

Tau schedule: Start with small tau (weights broad) to shape global, then larger to fine-tune peak. For sequence: tau = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0] relative to M? Using scale=M*tau. If tau small => scale small => weights concentrated on peak (narrow). Actually scale=M*tau; tau small => scale small => exp((c-M)/small) heavily weight near max (active narrow). tau large => broad. We want start broad: tau=1 or 2. Then narrow: 0.05. So schedule [1.0,0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02].

Maybe also use Euclidean gradient direction with normalized q? Multiplicative may be enough.

Simulated annealing perturb: If no improve after e.g. 20 iterations, add noise:
```
noise = rng.normal(0, sigma, n)
# apply low-pass? maybe just normalize
cand = current + sigma*noise
cand = np.clip(cand,0,None); cand/=cand.sum()
if score(cand) < best or random accept: current=cand
```
But random noise high frequency likely bad. Use smooth noise: generate random θ in low-frequency Fourier basis and add to log current: `logp = log(current+eps); add sum amp_k cos/sin random; p=softmax(logp)`. This preserves shape. Or mix with random good profiles.

Population approach may be easier for global: maintain list of best 20 from random profiles and DE, and periodically refine top.

### Use exact evaluator strategically

But if a candidate FFT looks best but exact is 2 due bug, final exact check ensures. We should not spend all time exact evaluating many. Use exact at start for height_sequence_1 and final.

The evaluator clips values to [0,1000] and scales invariant if sum >0.01. Our p sum1, values max maybe up to sqrt(M) ~0.014 for n=3809, all below 1000. OK.

### Could use `scipy.optimize` for full vector?

Maybe implement L-BFGS-B with gradient and projection? L-BFGS memory huge; but we could optimize a lower-dimensional embedding: p_i = softmax(W_i + parameters*random components). E.g., use a random low-rank basis B (n x K) with K=20. p=softmax(Bθ). This approximates full vector but lower dimension. Gradient of score wrt θ can be computed analytically? We can use autograd? No. But we can use numerical? Or use full vector gradient projected to low-rank (Julia). For a point in embedding, gradient in embedding = B.T * full_gradient. Use scipy L-BFGS-B or Nelder-Mead. This may optimize within a random subspace, good global. We could run multiple random bases K=20-50. But implementing L-BFGS with objective FFT convex? Objective nonconvex; L-BFGS may work local. It handles positivity via softmax. Parameter count 50, n=4096. Function evaluation 1 conv; gradient 1 conv+corr. L-BFGS iterations maybe few hundred, okay. But `minimize` with numerical? We can provide gradient. Need ensure no memory. Could use `scipy.optimize.minimize` method 'L-BFGS-B' if available. It might be robust. However, L-BFGS in a nonconvex landscape may fall into local; multiple bases. Also convergence can be slow. Mirror descent full vector may be simpler and more direct.

But L-BFGS on p directly (with bounds [0,1000] and sum constraint?) No, sum constraint can be handled by normalizing after each step, but optimizer expects fixed dims; normalization breaks gradient. Softmax parameterization handles sum1. p=softmax(z). Score invariant to z shift; non-identifiability. Could fix z0=0 or subtract mean in gradient. L-BFGS may handle. But p must be nonnegative; softmax positive. We can set z = Bθ + initial. Objective scale invariant so softmax sum1.

### Details of FFT convolution/correlation

```
def next_power_two(m): return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def conv_full_fft(a, n, L=None):
    if L is None: L=next_power_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    # remove small imag? irfft real. clip
    c[c<0] = 0.0  # but if invalid? no
    return c
```
For performance, precompute L in a context? Helper functions cannot close over but can accept L. We can create a ConvHelper? Top-level class? Rules say helper functions top level no closures; classes okay? Better just functions with parameters. In propose, precompute dictionary? We can pass L to each eval.

```
def score_fft(a, n, L):
    c=conv_full_fft(a,n,L)
    return float(2*n*c.max()) # if a sum1
```
If a not normalized, need divide by sum^2:
```
sum_a=a.sum()
if sum_a<0.01: return inf
c=... raw
return 2*n*c.max()/sum_a**2
```
For profiles raw sums not 1, use raw score. For optimization we can normalize to1 to keep scale.

Correlation:
```
def corr_valid_fft(q,p,n,Lcorr=None):
    # q length 2n-1, p length n
    if Lcorr is None: Lcorr=next_power_two(3*n-2)
    qf=np.fft.rfft(q,Lcorr)
    pref=p[::-1]
    pf=np.fft.rfft(pref,Lcorr)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(qf*pf,Lcorr)
    d=conv[n-1:2*n-1]
    return np.maximum(d,0.0)
```
Check orientation and length. For n=1: q len1, p len1, L>=2? 3n-2=1, L=1? next_power_two(1)=1 (1<<0). rfft length1 okay; conv len1? n-1:2*n-1 ->0:1 works.
For n=0 invalid.

But FFT convolution can introduce tiny negative d; clip.

### Refinement Function in Detail

Let's design a robust `refine_vector(p, n, rng, max_time, verbose)` that uses mirror/mix updates with adaptive τ and beta/mix. It should return best p and score.

Parameters:
```
def refine_vector(p, n, rng, max_time, initial_tau=0.5, verbose=True):
    deadline = time.monotonic()+max_time
    current = clip+normalize(p)
    current_score = score_fft_norm(current,n,L)
    best = current.copy(); best_score=current_score
    t_since_improve=0
    tau_cycle = [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01]
    tau_idx=0
    no_iter=0
    iter=0
    while time<deadline:
       c = conv_full_fft(current,n,Lconv)
       M = c.max()
       if not finite: break
       scale = max(M*tau, 1e-15)
       x = (c - M)/scale
       # stable weights
       x = np.clip(x, -50, 0)
       q = np.exp(x)
       qsum=q.sum()
       if qsum<=0: q = np.zeros; q[argmax]=1
       q/=qsum
       d = corr_valid_fft(q,current,n,Lcorr)
       d = np.maximum(d, 1e-300)
       mean_d = d.mean()
       best_cand_score = current_score
       best_cand = current.copy()
       improved=False
       # candidate modes
       # ratio updates
       for beta in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]:
           factor = np.power(mean_d / d, beta)
           factor = np.clip(factor, 0.05, 20.0)
           cand = current * factor
           cand = add_floor+normalize
           s=score_fft_norm(cand,n,Lconv)
           if s<best_cand_score: ...
       # inverse mix updates
       # inv_p = 1 / d**beta normalized; but d may vary.
       for beta in [0.3,0.6,1.0]:
           inv = np.power(d, -beta)
           # To avoid huge, clip inv to [1/max_inv, 10]?
           inv = np.clip(inv, 0.1, 100.0)
           inv /= inv.sum()
           for mix in [0.1,0.3,0.6]:
               # linear mix
               cand = (1-mix)*current + mix*inv
               cand = clip positive normalize
               evaluate
               # logmix maybe
               logc = (1-mix)*np.log(current+1e-300) + mix*(np.log(inv+1e-300))
               cand = softmax(logc)
               evaluate
       if best_cand_score < current_score-1e-12:
           current = best_cand; current_score=best_cand_score; t=0; update best
       else:
           no_iter++
           # Maybe try negative direction? If score not improving, maybe current in basin; perturb.
           if no_iter > 10:
               current = perturb_smooth(current, step=0.05*sigma, rng)
               current_score = score_fft_norm(...)
               # If worse than best but accept to explore? yes set current to perturbation if finite.
               no_iter=0
           # Update tau occasionally
       # schedule tau: if improved maybe after some iters move to next tau
       if (iter % 20 ==0): tau_idx=(tau_idx+1)%len(tau_cycle); tau=tau_cycle[tau_idx]
       iter++
```
This generates up to 5+3*(3+3)=26 candidates per iteration; too many. Need balance. We can reduce: 3 ratio betas, 2 inverse mixes. Maybe 8 candidates. 8 FFTs per iter. For n=8192, 8*0.5ms=4ms, 10k=40s; fine. Use 10 candidates.

But line search evaluating many candidates from same d: score_fft each does conv_full_fft O(nlogn). Could be heavy. We can approximate candidate score using current c plus quadratic? But maybe not.

Alternative use exact max from FFT for only chosen candidate after a cheap approximation. But okay.

Candidate normalization: p sum1; if some values become tiny, set floor maybe `cand += 1e-15` then normalize. But adding floor changes support; okay.

Perturb_smooth:
```
def smooth_perturb(p, n, rng, strength):
   # add low-frequency random to log p
   logp=np.log(p+1e-15)
   # generate random Fourier up to max_freq=10
   coeffs = rng.normal(0, strength, size=(freq,2))
   delta = zeros
   for m in 1..freq: delta += a cos(2π m x)+b sin(...)
   # also random linear tilt small
   newlog=logp + delta
   cand=softmax(newlog)
   return cand
```
Strength adapt: 0.05, 0.1, 0.2. If objective new < best*1.05 maybe accept; else revert. This explores.

Maybe better to use "restart from best with noise" if no improve: `cand = normalize(best + noise*0.1*best)` etc.

### Avoid Symmetry

Initial seeds should be asymmetric. During updates, if current becomes nearly symmetric by chance, the objective might rise to 2. We can include a symmetry-breaking regularizer? Not in objective, but if local min symmetric score2, perturbations should break. We can monitor asymmetry `asym = norm(p - p[::-1])/(norm(p))`. If asym too low and score near 2, strongly perturb asym (add linear trend). But optimum likely asymmetric. Add a small penalty for symmetry? That might bias away from true optimum if it were symmetric (but impossible <2). We can add a small tie-breaker only if score near 2: subtract ε*||p-p_rev||^2? Not necessary.

### Implement softmax stable

```
def softmax_like(logv):
    z=np.asarray(logv,float)
    z=max(z - max, -700)
    v=np.exp(z)
    s=v.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return uniform
    return v/s
```

### Implement perturbation

```
def smooth_perturb(p, n, rng, strength, max_freq=8):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-15))
    delta=np.zeros(n)
    for m in range(1,max_freq+1):
       a=rng.normal(0, strength/(m**0.5))
       b=rng.normal(0, strength/(m**0.5))
       delta += a*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x) + b*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x)
    # add tilt
    delta += rng.normal(0, strength)*x
    return softmax(logp+delta)
```
This can introduce asymmetry. If p has zeros, log floor.

Maybe also "block perturb" like previous? But mirror/differential likely better.

### Seed generation details

```
def initial_seeds(n, rng, parent=None):
   seeds=[]
   # uniform but add tiny asymmetry? Uniform score2 and symmetric; add linear small.
   for a in [0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,1.0]:
      x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
      u=1 + a*(2*x-1)
      u=np.maximum(u,1e-6)
      seeds.append(u/sum)
   # quadratic grid
   for a in [0.3,0.5,0.7]: for b in [-0.3,0,0.3]:
      u=1 + a*(2x-1) + b*(2x-1)**2; clip
   # exponential ramps
   for g in [0.2,0.5,1,2,5,10]:
      u=(x+1e-3)**g; also reverse
   # random smooth profiles
   for _ in range(20):
      p=smooth_perturb(uniform, n, rng, strength=1.0) # maybe log random
   if parent: resample parent
   # two level
   for theta in [0.3,0.5,0.7]: for h in [0.3,0.6,1,2]: u = h if x<theta else1
   # random binary with uncorrelated?
   for _ in range(5):
       m=n//2; idx=np.random.choice(n,m,replace=False); u=zeros; u[idx]=1; normalize
   # low-discrepancy binary? maybe use periodic pattern with period 3: [1,0,1,0,1,0]? score? Could include.
```

def resample_sequence(parent, n):

   else:

For inverse update:
```
for beta in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
    inv = np.power(d_safe + 1e-300, -beta)
    # normalize inv; if d large, inv small; stable via log
    logd = np.log(d_safe)
    loginv = -beta*logd
    loginv = loginv - loginv.max()
    inv=np.exp(loginv); inv/=inv.sum()
    for mix in (0.05,0.2,0.5):
       # linear
       cand = (1-mix)*current + mix*inv
       cand=np.maximum(cand,0); normalize eval
       # logmix
       logc = (1-mix)*np.log(np.maximum(current,1e-300)) - mix*beta*np.log(d_safe) # because log inv
       # but log current may be very negative; stable softmax
       logc -= logc.max(); cand=softmax; eval
```

```
def try_candidate(cand, current_score, best_score, best_cand, n, Lconv, floor=1e-15):
    cand=np.asarray(cand,float)
    if not finite: return None
    cand[cand<0]=0
    s=cand.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    cand /= s
    cand += floor
    cand /= cand.sum()
    sc=score_normalized(cand,n,Lconv)
    return sc,cand
```

```
def optimize_profile(n, Lconv, rng, time_limit):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    B=np.vstack([...])
    K=B.shape[1]
    theta=rng.normal(0,0.5,K)
    p=softmax(B@theta); score=...
    step=0.3
    deadline=...
    no_improve=0
    while time:
       improved=False
       for j in range(K):
          for delta in (-step,-step/2,step/2,step):
             theta2=theta.copy(); theta2[j]+=delta
             if abs(theta2[j])>5: continue
             p2=softmax(B@theta2); sc2=score...
             if sc2<score-1e-12:
                 theta=theta2; p=p2; score=sc2; improved=True; no_improve=0; break
          if improved: break
       if not improved:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>5:
             theta = theta + rng.normal(0,0.2,K)
          if step>0.05: step*=0.7
          else: restart with theta=rng.normal(0,1,K)
       # check time
    return p, score
```
This evaluates many. For n=8192, L=16384; each score ~0.5ms; 500 evals 0.25s? Actually Python loops with FFT maybe 1ms, 500=0.5s. Good. We can set time limit 20s per n. It might improve seeds. But if nonconvex, coordinate may get stuck; random restarts.

def _pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

#### optimize_profile no lambdas

```
def optimize_profile(n,Lconv,rng,time_limit):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    B=np.vstack((x, x*x, x*x*x, x*x*x*x, np.cos(np.pi*x), np.cos(2*np.pi*x), np.sin(np.pi*x)))
    K=B.shape[1]
    theta=rng.normal(0,0.7,K)
    z=B.dot(theta); p=softmax(z); score=score_normalized_fft(p,n,Lconv)
    best= (score,p.copy())
    step=0.35
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    no=0
    restarts=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       improved=False
       for j in range(K):
          for delta in (-step, -0.5*step, 0.5*step, step, -2*step, 2*step):
             if time>deadline: break
             theta2=theta.copy(); theta2[j]+=delta
             if abs(theta2[j])>6: continue
             z2=B.dot(theta2); p2=softmax(z2); sc2=score...
             if sc2 < score-1e-12:
                 theta=theta2; p=p2; score=sc2; improved=True; no=0
                 if sc2 < best[0]: best=(sc2,p.copy())
                 break
          if improved or time>deadline: break
       if not improved:
          no+=1
          if no>4:
             step = max(0.05, step*0.75)
             if step <=0.05 and restarts < 10:
                theta = best[1]? can't get theta from p. Keep best_theta.
                theta = best_theta.copy() + rng.normal(0,1,K)
                z=B.dot(theta); p=softmax(z); score=...
                restarts+=1
             no=0
       # maybe update best_theta when improved
    return best
```
Need store best_theta. Initialize best_theta=theta.copy(); on improvement update.

Time limit for profile maybe 10s; if no improvement multiple restarts. Could be heavy but ok.

We need avoid using lambda in sort; import operator.

### refine_vector code details

```
def refine_vector(p,n,Lconv,Lcorr,rng,time_limit):
    current=normalize_nonneg(p)
    current_score=score_normalized_fft(current,n,Lconv)
    best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score
    tau_list=(1.0,0.6,0.35,0.2,0.12,0.07,0.04,0.02)
    tau_idx=0
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max(1.0,time_limit)
    no_iter=0
    iter=0
    pert_strength=0.1
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        c=conv_full_fft(current,n,Lconv)
        M=float(c.max())
        if not math.isfinite(M) or M<=0:
            current = smooth_perturb(current,n,rng,0.2); current_score=...; continue
        tau=tau_list[min(tau_idx,len(tau_list)-1)]
        scale=max(M*tau, 1e-15)
        xvals=(c-M)/scale
        xvals=np.where(np.isfinite(xvals), xvals, -745.0)
        # To keep qsum reasonable, maybe subtract min? Since all <=0.
        expx=np.exp(xvals)
        s=expx.sum()
        if s<=0 or not finite:
           q=np.zeros(2*n-1); q[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
        else: q=expx/s
        d=corr_valid_fft(q,current,n,Lcorr)
        d=np.maximum(d, 1e-300)
        mean_d=float(d.mean())
        if not finite or mean_d<=0: continue
        local_best_score=current_score; local_best_cand=current.copy()
        # candidates
        # ratio
        for beta in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4):
            factor=np.power(mean_d/d, beta)
            factor=np.clip(factor,0.05,20.0)
            cand=current*factor
            cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0)
            cs=cand.sum()
            if cs>0 and finite:
                cand/=cs; cand+=1e-15; cand/=cand.sum()
                sc=score_normalized_fft(cand,n,Lconv)
                update local
        # logmix inverse
        logp=np.log(np.maximum(current,1e-300))
        logd=np.log(np.maximum(d,1e-300))
        for beta in (0.3,0.7,1.2):
            for mix in (0.1,0.3,0.6):
                logc=(1.0-mix)*logp - mix*beta*logd
                cand=softmax_log(logc)
                sc=...
                update local
        # maybe linear mix with inv for beta=0.7
        # inv via softmax_log(-0.7*logd)
        inv=softmax_log(-0.7*logd)
        for mix in (0.05,0.2):
            cand=(1.0-mix)*current + mix*inv
            cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0); normalize eval
        if local_best_score < current_score - 1e-12:
            current=local_best_cand; current_score=local_best_score
            no_iter=0
            if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score
        else:
            no_iter += 1
            if no_iter >= 12:
                # perturb
                for strength in (pert_strength, pert_strength*2, 0.05):
                    cand=smooth_perturb(current,n,rng,strength)
                    sc=score_normalized_fft(cand,n,Lconv)
                    if sc < best_score + 0.3 and (sc < current_score+0.2 or rng.random()<0.1):
                        current=cand; current_score=sc; break
                pert_strength = min(1.0, pert_strength*1.2)
                no_iter=0
                tau_idx = max(0, tau_idx-2)  # broaden
        if iter % 10 == 9:
            tau_idx += 1
            if tau_idx > len(tau_list)-1: tau_idx=len(tau_list)-1
        iter += 1
        if iter % 50 == 0: print progress
    return best,best_score
```

### Smooth perturb implementation

```
def smooth_perturb(p,n,rng,strength=0.1,max_freq=6):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    delta=np.zeros(n)
    for m in range(1,max_freq+1):
        amp=strength/(m**0.7)
        a=rng.normal(0,amp); b=rng.normal(0,amp)
        delta += a*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x) + b*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x)
    # linear/random tilt
    delta += rng.normal(0, strength*0.5)*(2*x-1)
    return softmax_log(logp+delta)
```
This can change sum via softmax. Good.

def run_length(n,time_limit,rng,parent):
    Lconv=next_pow2(2*n-1); Lcorr=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    profile_time = min(30.0, max(5.0, time_limit*0.05)) if time_limit>100 else 0
    seeds=generate_seeds(n,Lconv,rng,parent,profile_time=max(0, min(30, deadline-time.monotonic()-5)))

    # top seeds 4

    # chunks

    for idx in range(num):
       remaining=deadline-time.monotonic()

          chunk = max(10, remaining/(num-idx+0.5)) # maybe

       if s2<best_score: ...

### Softmax stability in candidate logmix

Implement:
```
def softmax_log(logv):
   z=np.asarray(logv,float)
   if z.size==0: return np.array([])
   if np.all(~np.isfinite(z)): return np.full(z.shape,1/z.size)
   finite_mask=np.isfinite(z)
   if not finite_mask.all(): z[~finite_mask] = -745.0
   m=float(z.max())
   if not math.isfinite(m): return uniform
   z=z-m
   v=np.exp(z)
   s=v.sum()
   if s<=0... uniform
   return v/s
```
No closures.

def evaluate_candidate(cand, n, Lconv):
    cand = np.asarray(cand, dtype=float)
    if cand.size == 0: return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(cand)): return None
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    budget = float(budget_s)
    if not math.isfinite(budget) or budget <=0: budget=2400
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(10.0,budget) - 30.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    parent = get_parent()
    parent_arr = None
    if parent is not None:
       try:
          parent_arr = np.asarray(parent, dtype=float)
          if parent_arr.ndim !=1 or parent_arr.size==0: parent_arr=None
          else: parent_arr = np.where(np.isfinite(parent_arr), parent_arr, 0.0)
       except: parent_arr=None
    candidates=[]
    # add parent exact? Maybe add constant fallback to candidates later.
    if parent_arr is not None and parent_arr.size>0:
       n_parent = min(len(parent_arr), 3809?)
       p_parent = normalize_nonneg(parent_arr)
       # For final exact, add parent list; for FFT, add if length okay? parent length 3809.
       candidates.append((score_normalized_fft(p_parent, len(p_parent), next_pow2(2*len(p_parent)-1)), p_parent.copy(), len(p_parent)))
    # runs
    runs=[]
    if budget > 1000:
       t1=int(min(600, (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.25))
       t2=int(min(budget-100, (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.70))
       runs=[(3809,t1),(8192,t2)]
    else:
       runs=[(3809,int(max(10,deadline-time.monotonic())))]
    for n, t in runs:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60: break
       t = min(t, int(deadline - time.monotonic() - 30))
       if t < 30: continue
       print(f"[propose] starting n={n} time={t}")
       p_best, s_best = run_length(n, t, rng, parent_arr)
       if p_best is not None and s_best < inf:
           candidates.append((float(s_best), p_best.copy(), int(p_best.size)))
       # if time left, exact check? maybe at end
    # exact check candidates
    candidates.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    best_exact = inf; best_list = None
    max_checks = 6
    for i in range(max_checks):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10: break
       if i >= len(candidates): break
       _, p, n = candidates[i]
       # To avoid duplicate long exact, check unique n maybe
       seq = [float(x) for x in p]
       ex = exact_score_list(seq)
       if math.isfinite(ex) and ex < best_exact:
           best_exact = ex; best_list = seq
       print(f"[exact] candidate {i} n={n} fft={candidates[i][0]:.6f} exact={ex:.6f}")
    if best_list is None:
       best_list = fallback_sequence()
       best_exact = exact_score_list(best_list) # maybe 2
    return best_list

`resample_sequence`:
```
def resample_sequence(parent,n):
   arr=np.asarray(parent,float)
   if arr.ndim!=1: arr=arr.ravel()
   if arr.size==0: return None
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr,0.0)
   if arr.size == n: out=arr
   else:
      x0=np.linspace(0,1,arr.size); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n)
      out=np.interp(x1,x0,arr)
   return normalize_nonneg(out)
```

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(a*a)/(sum(a))**2
using FFT-based evaluations, profile-based seeding, and a smoothed
multiplicative descent in the simplex.  The final sequence is checked
with the provided evaluate_sequence() oracle when available.
"""

import time
import math
import operator

import numpy as np


def _next_pow2(m: int) -> int:
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize_nonneg(a) -> np.ndarray:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a) & (a > 0.0), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1e12)
    s = float(np.sum(a, dtype=np.float64))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s
    return a


def _conv_full_fft(a: np.ndarray, n: int, L: int) -> np.ndarray:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    c = c[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.real(c)
    c = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)
    return c


def _score_normalized_fft(a: np.ndarray, n: int, L: int) -> float:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size != n:
        return float("inf")
    c = _conv_full_fft(a, n, L)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(c.max())
    if not math.isfinite(mx) or mx < 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def _corr_valid_fft(q: np.ndarray, p: np.ndarray, n: int, L: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    d[i] = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} p[j] * q[i+j]
    for i=0..n-1, where q has length 2*n-1.
    """
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    qf = np.fft.rfft(q, L)
    pref = p[::-1]
    pf = np.fft.rfft(pref, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(qf * pf, L)
    d = conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    d = np.real(d)
    d = np.clip(d, 0.0, None)
    return d


def _softmax_log(logv) -> np.ndarray:
    z = np.asarray(logv, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(z.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -745.0)

    m = float(np.max(z))
    if not math.isfinite(m):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)

    z = z - m
    v = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(v, dtype=np.float64))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return v / s


def _candidate_score(cand, n: int, L: int):
    cand = np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64)
    if cand.size != n:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(cand)):
        return None

    cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(cand, dtype=np.float64))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return None

    cand = cand / s
    cand += 1e-15
    s2 = float(np.sum(cand, dtype=np.float64))
    if s2 <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s2):
        return None
    cand = cand / s2

    sc = _score_normalized_fft(cand, n, L)
    if not math.isfinite(sc):
        return None
    return sc, cand


def _smooth_perturb(p: np.ndarray, n: int, rng, strength: float, max_freq: int = 7) -> np.ndarray:
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    delta = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for m in range(1, max_freq + 1):
        amp = float(strength) / (float(m) ** 0.7)
        a = float(rng.normal(0.0, amp))
        b = float(rng.normal(0.0, amp))
        delta += a * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * x) + b * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * x)

    delta += float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5 * max(strength, 1e-6))) * (2.0 * x - 1.0)
    return _softmax_log(logp + delta)


def _resample_sequence(parent, n: int):
    arr = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None

    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr > 0.0), arr, 0.0)
    if arr.size == n:
        return _normalize_nonneg(arr)

    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.interp(x1, x0, arr)
    return _normalize_nonneg(out)


def _optimize_profile(n: int, L: int, rng, time_limit: float):
    if time_limit <= 1.0:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
        return _normalize_nonneg(1.0 + 0.4 * (2.0 * x - 1.0)), _score_normalized_fft(
            _normalize_nonneg(1.0 + 0.4 * (2.0 * x - 1.0)), n, L
        )

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    basis = np.vstack(
        [
            x,
            x * x,
            x * x * x,
            x * x * x * x,
            np.cos(np.pi * x),
            np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x),
            np.sin(np.pi * x),
        ]
    ).astype(np.float64)
    K = int(basis.shape[1])

    theta = rng.normal(0.0, 0.7, size=K).astype(np.float64)
    p = _softmax_log(basis.dot(theta))
    score = _score_normalized_fft(p, n, L)
    if not math.isfinite(score):
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
        score = 2.0
        theta = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = score
    best_theta = theta.copy()
    step = 0.35
    no_improve = 0
    restarts = 0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_limit)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        improved = False

        for j in range(K):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            deltas = (-step, -0.5 * step, 0.5 * step, step, -2.0 * step, 2.0 * step)
            for delta in deltas:
                theta2 = theta.copy()
                theta2[j] += delta
                if abs(theta2[j]) > 7.0:
                    continue
                z = basis.dot(theta2)
                p2 = _softmax_log(z)
                sc2 = _score_normalized_fft(p2, n, L)
                if math.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < score - 1e-13:
                    theta = theta2
                    p = p2
                    score = sc2
                    improved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                    if sc2 < best_score:
                        best_score = sc2
                        best_p = p.copy()
                        best_theta = theta.copy()
                    break
            if improved:
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve >= 5:
                step = max(0.05, step * 0.78)
                if restarts < 14:
                    theta = best_theta + rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=K)
                    z = basis.dot(theta)
                    p = _softmax_log(z)
                    score = _score_normalized_fft(p, n, L)
                    if not math.isfinite(score):
                        theta = best_theta.copy()
                        p = best_p.copy()
                        score = best_score
                    restarts += 1
                    no_improve = 0

    return best_p, best_score


def _generate_seeds(n: int, L: int, rng, parent, profile_time: float, deadline: float):
    seeds = []

    def add(arr):
        p = _normalize_nonneg(arr)
        if p.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            seeds.append(p)

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 2.0 * x - 1.0

    # Asymmetric smooth deterministic seeds.
    for a in (0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 0.55, 0.75, 1.0, 1.4):
        add(1.0 + a * t)
        add(1.0 - a * t)

    for a in (-0.6, -0.3, 0.0, 0.3, 0.6):
        for b in (-0.5, -0.2, 0.0, 0.2, 0.5):
            add(1.0 + a * t + b * t * t)

    # Exponential / log-polynomial seeds.
    for g in (-3.0, -2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        add(np.exp(g * (x - 0.5)))
        add(np.exp(g * (1.0 - x)))

    for a in (-3.0, -2.0, -1.0, 0.0, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0):
        for b in (-3.0, -2.0, -1.0, 0.0, 1.0, 2.0):
            add(np.exp(a * x + b * x * x - (a + 2.0 * b) * 0.5))

    # Two/three level skewed seeds.
    for theta in (0.35, 0.5, 0.65):
        for h in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            u = np.where(x < theta, h, 1.0)
            add(u)
            u2 = np.where(x < theta, 1.0, h)
            add(u2)

    # Random smooth seeds.
    for _ in range(24):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2:
            break
        add(_smooth_perturb(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n, n, rng, 1.0, 7))

    # Parent, if available.
    if parent is not None:
        rp = _resample_sequence(parent, n)
        if rp is not None:
            add(rp)
            add(rp[::-1])

    # A few sparse masks; these are usually not top-rated but can seed basins.
    for _ in range(6):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2:
            break
        m = max(1, n // 2)
        u = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
        u[idx] = 1.0
        add(u)
        add(u[::-1])

    # Profile optimization seeds.
    if profile_time > 3.0:
        tries = 3
        per = max(1.0, float(profile_time) / max(1, tries))
        for _ in range(tries):
            rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 4.0)
            pt = min(per, rem)
            if pt <= 2.0:
                break
            pp, ss = _optimize_profile(n, L, rng, pt)
            if pp is not None and math.isfinite(ss):
                add(pp)

    if not seeds:
        uniform = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
        return [uniform]

    scored = []
    for p in seeds:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 1:
            break
        sc = _score_normalized_fft(p, n, L)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, p))

    if not scored:
        return [np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n]

    scored.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    out = [p for _, p in scored[:12]]
    return out


def _refine_vector(p0: np.ndarray, n: int, Lconv: int, Lcorr: int, rng, time_limit: float):
    if time_limit <= 2.0:
        p = _normalize_nonneg(p0)
        return p, _score_normalized_fft(p, n, Lconv)

    current = _normalize_nonneg(p0)
    current_score = _score_normalized_fft(current, n, Lconv)
    if not math.isfinite(current_score):
        current = _smooth_perturb(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n, n, rng, 0.5)
        current_score = _score_normalized_fft(current, n, Lconv)

    best = current.copy()
    best_score = current_score

    tau_values = (1.0, 0.65, 0.4, 0.25, 0.15, 0.09, 0.05, 0.03, 0.015)
    tau_idx = 0
    no_improve = 0
    perturb_strength = 0.12
    it = 0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_limit)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = _conv_full_fft(current, n, Lconv)
        M = float(c.max())
        if not math.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            current = _smooth_perturb(current, n, rng, 0.35)
            current_score = _score_normalized_fft(current, n, Lconv)
            continue

        tau = float(tau_values[min(tau_idx, len(tau_values) - 1)])
        scale = max(M * tau, 1e-15)
        xvals = (c - M) / scale
        xvals = np.where(np.isfinite(xvals), xvals, -745.0)
        w = np.exp(xvals)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
            q = np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=np.float64)
            q[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            q = w / ws

        d = _corr_valid_fft(q, current, n, Lcorr)
        d = np.maximum(d, 1e-300)
        mean_d = float(np.mean(d))
        if not math.isfinite(mean_d) or mean_d <= 0.0:
            d = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            mean_d = 1.0 / n

        local_best_score = current_score
        local_best_cand = current.copy()

        # Ratio update: reduce coordinates with large contribution.
        for beta in (0.05, 0.15, 0.35):
            ratio = mean_d / d
            ratio = np.clip(ratio, 1e-3, 1e3)
            factor = np.power(ratio, beta)
            factor = np.clip(factor, 0.05, 20.0)
            cand = current * factor
            res = _candidate_score(cand, n, Lconv)
            if res is not None:
                sc, pc = res
                if sc < local_best_score:
                    local_best_score = sc
                    local_best_cand = pc

        # Log-mixed inverse updates, similar to p -> p / d^beta with damping.
        logp = np.log(np.maximum(current, 1e-300))
        logd = np.log(np.maximum(d, 1e-300))
        for beta in (0.5, 1.0):
            for mix in (0.2, 0.5, 0.9):
                logc = (1.0 - mix) * logp - mix * beta * logd
                cand = _softmax_log(logc)
                res = _candidate_score(cand, n, Lconv)
                if res is not None:
                    sc, pc = res
                    if sc < local_best_score:
                        local_best_score = sc
                        local_best_cand = pc

        # One linear mix toward a pure inverse-gradient direction.
        inv = _softmax_log(-0.7 * logd)
        for mix in (0.08, 0.25):
            cand = (1.0 - mix) * current + mix * inv
            res = _candidate_score(cand, n, Lconv)
            if res is not None:
                sc, pc = res
                if sc < local_best_score:
                    local_best_score = sc
                    local_best_cand = pc

        if local_best_score < current_score - 1e-12:
            current = local_best_cand
            current_score = local_best_score
            no_improve = 0
            if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = current_score
                best = current.copy()
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve >= 12:
                accepted = False
                for strength in (perturb_strength, perturb_strength * 2.0, 0.05):
                    cand = _smooth_perturb(current, n, rng, strength, 8)
                    sc = _score_normalized_fft(cand, n, Lconv)
                    if math.isfinite(sc) and (sc < best_score + 0.35 or rng.random() < 0.08):
                        current = cand
                        current_score = sc
                        accepted = True
                        break
                if not accepted:
                    current = best.copy()
                    current_score = best_score
                perturb_strength = min(1.0, perturb_strength * 1.25)
                tau_idx = max(0, tau_idx - 2)
                no_improve = 0

        if it % 12 == 11:
            tau_idx += 1
            if tau_idx > len(tau_values) - 1:
                tau_idx = len(tau_values) - 1

        if it % 60 == 0:
            print(
                f"[refine] n={n} iter={it} current={current_score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f} tau={tau:.3f}",
                flush=True,
            )

        it += 1

    return best, best_score


def _run_one_length(n: int, time_limit: float, rng, parent):
    n = int(n)
    if n < 2:
        n = 8
    Lconv = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Lcorr = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(time_limit)

    profile_time = 0.0
    if time_limit > 240:
        profile_time = min(45.0, max(8.0, time_limit * 0.05))
    elif time_limit > 60:
        profile_time = min(12.0, max(2.0, time_limit * 0.08))

    seeds = _generate_seeds(n, Lconv, rng, parent, profile_time, deadline - 5.0)
    if not seeds:
        seeds = [np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n]

    best_p = seeds[0].copy()
    best_s = _score_normalized_fft(best_p, n, Lconv)
    if not math.isfinite(best_s):
        best_s = 2.0

    top = seeds[:4]
    # Fractions sum to slightly less than 1; leftover is final polish.
    fracs = (0.42, 0.28, 0.16, 0.08)
    for i, seed_p in enumerate(top):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 25:
            break
        rem = float(deadline - time.monotonic())
        if rem < 25:
            break
        if i < len(fracs):
            chunk = min(rem - 25.0, rem * fracs[i])
        else:
            chunk = 10.0
        if chunk < 10.0:
            continue
        p2, s2 = _refine_vector(seed_p, n, Lconv, Lcorr, rng, chunk)
        if s2 < best_s:
            best_s = s2
            best_p = p2.copy()
        print(f"[run] n={n} seed_refine best_fft={best_s:.6f} time_left={deadline - time.monotonic():.1f}", flush=True)

    # Final polish on the best found so far.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 70:
        rem = max(0.0, float(deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0))
        if rem > 70:
            p2, s2 = _refine_vector(best_p, n, Lconv, Lcorr, rng, rem * 0.92)
            if s2 < best_s:
                best_s = s2
                best_p = p2.copy()
            print(f"[run] n={n} polish best_fft={best_s:.6f}", flush=True)

    return best_p, best_s


def _exact_score_list(seq_list) -> float:
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq_list)  # assumed to be in global namespace
        if val is None:
            return float("inf")
        val = float(val)
        if math.isnan(val) or math.isinf(val):
            return float("inf")
        return val
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _fallback_sequence():
    # Constant sequence gives the trivial value 2 (or 2 + floating error).
    return [1.0] * 3809


def _get_parent_sequence():
    val = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    return val


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.
    Returns a Python list of nonnegative floats.
    """
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    if not math.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0.0:
        budget = 2400.0

    reserve = 120.0 if budget > 1200.0 else max(60.0, 0.15 * budget)
    reserve = min(reserve, 180.0)
    wall_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = wall_start + max(5.0, budget - reserve)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if np.isfinite(float(seed)) else 42)
    parent = _get_parent_sequence()
    parent_arr = None
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            pa = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
            if pa.ndim != 1 or pa.size == 0:
                parent_arr = None
            else:
                parent_arr = pa
        except Exception:
            parent_arr = None

    candidates = []

    # Include the supplied parent as an exact-check candidate, if valid.
    if parent_arr is not None:
        pp = _normalize_nonneg(parent_arr)
        if pp.size >= 2:
            npar = int(pp.size)
            Lpar = _next_pow2(2 * npar - 1)
            s = _score_normalized_fft(pp, npar, Lpar)
            if math.isfinite(s):
                candidates.append((s, pp.copy(), npar))

    # Choose lengths and time allocations.
    runs = []
    rem0 = max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    if rem0 > 1200.0:
        # Primary known length plus a larger low-risk length.
        t1 = int(min(560.0, rem0 * 0.27))
        t2 = int(min(rem0 - 240.0, rem0 * 0.67))
        if t2 < 240:
            t2 = int(rem0 * 0.7)
        if t1 > 30:
            runs.append((3809, max(60, t1)))
        if t2 > 240:
            runs.append((8192, t2))
    elif rem0 > 400.0:
        runs.append((3809, int(max(120.0, rem0 - 60))))
    else:
        runs.append((3809, int(max(20.0, rem0 - 30))))

    for n, t in runs:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 45:
            break
        t = min(int(t), int(max(30.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 45)))
        if t < 30:
            continue

        print(f"[propose] length={n} budget_secs={t}", flush=True)
        best_p, best_s = _run_one_length(n, t, rng, parent_arr)

        if best_p is not None and best_p.size > 0:
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(best_p)):
                best_p = np.where(np.isfinite(best_p), best_p, 0.0)
                best_p = _normalize_nonneg(best_p)
            sc = _score_normalized_fft(best_p, int(best_p.size), _next_pow2(2 * int(best_p.size) - 1))
            if math.isfinite(sc):
                candidates.append((sc, best_p.copy(), int(best_p.size)))

    # Exact check top candidates.
    if not candidates:
        fb = _fallback_sequence()
        _exact_score_list(fb)
        return fb

    candidates.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

    best_exact = float("inf")
    best_list = None
    checked_n = set()
    max_checks = 8

    for i in range(min(max_checks, len(candidates))):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 15:
            break
        _, p, n = candidates[i]
        if n in checked_n and i > 0:
            continue
        checked_n.add(n)

        try:
            seq = [float(v) for v in p]
        except Exception:
            continue

        ex = _exact_score_list(seq)
        print(f"[exact] top_fft={i} n={n} fft={candidates[i][0]:.8f} exact={ex:.8f}", flush=True)
        if math.isfinite(ex) and ex < best_exact:
            best_exact = ex
            best_list = seq

    if best_list is None:
        # Should not happen, but provide a valid fallback.
        fb = _fallback_sequence()
        best_list = fb

    return best_list
```
